M_{P}=2(\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]+\mathbb{E}_{P}[w]C_{1}+C_{2})
.

To evaluate the second term, define

b_{t}=C(t^{q-1}\lor\sqrt{2}t^{-2})
, where
C=c_{q-1}(1+\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}^{2(q-1)}]^{1/2}
.
The constant
b_{t}
represents a bound on the maximum of (weighted) derivatives of
f_{t}
to guarantee
b_{t}^{-1}f_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{q}
(see
K.5
).
The second term is thus bounded as

\left\lvert\int f_{t}\differential(P-Q)\right\rvert\leq b_{t}d_{\mathcal{F}_{q}}(P,Q).

The estimates above yield, with
D=d_{\mathcal{F}_{q}}(P,Q)
,

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{PL}_{q-1}}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{t>0}M_{P}(t\lor t^{q})+BC_{1}Dt+b_{t}D

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{t>0}M_{P}t(1\lor t^{q-1})+BC_{1}Dt+\sqrt{2}CDt^{-2}(1\lor t^{q+1})

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{P}t_{*}(1\lor t^{q+1})+BC_{1}Dt_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}C}{M_{P}}\right)^{1/3}\left[2M_{P}\left\{1\lor\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}C}{M_{P}}D\right)^{(q+1)/3}\right\}+BC_{1}D\right]D^{1/3},

where, in the third line,
t_{*}
is chosen such that
M_{P}t_{*}=\sqrt{2}CDt_{*}^{-2}
.
∎

Appendix G
KSD bounds on pseudo-Lipschitz metric

Here, we collect proofs of

3.2
,
3.3
and
3.4
,
which provide KSD bounds on the pseudo-Lipschitz metric
d_{\mathcal{F}_{q}}
(see their respective proofs in
Sections

G.3
,
G.3.4
and
G.3.5
).
In the following, without loss of generality, we assume
Q\in{\cal P}_{q}
, since otherwise both sides of the inequalities (in
3.2
,
3.3
and
3.4
) become
\infty
.

For ease of presentation, we restate the proof strategy. If we assume
the existence of a solution
v_{f}
to the Stein equation (
10
),
we can obtain a bound on the worst-case expectation error with respect
to
{\cal F}_{q}:

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q)=\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{q}}\left|\int f\differential P-\int f\differential Q\right|\leq\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{q}}\left|\int{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}\differential Q\right|.

Our goal is to express the upper bound in terms of the KSD. To this
end, we approximate each
v_{f}
by an RKHS function
\tilde{v}_{f}\in{\cal H}_{K}
,
which yields an estimate

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\int\left|{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-{\cal T}_{P}^{m}\tilde{v}_{f}\right|\differential Q+\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\lVert\tilde{v}_{f}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\cdot\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\underbrace{\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r}\left(\lvert{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}\rvert+\lvert{\cal T}_{P}^{m}\tilde{v}_{f}\rvert\right)\differential Q}_{\mathrm{(A)}}+\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\underbrace{\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left|{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-{\cal T}_{P}^{m}\tilde{v}_{f}\right|\differential Q}_{\mathrm{(B)}}

\displaystyle\vphantom{\leq}\quad+\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\underbrace{\lVert\tilde{v}_{f}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}}_{(\mathrm{C})}\cdot\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q).

Each term is evaluated as follows. In Lemmas
G.1

G.2
, we show that the term (A) can be expressed
in terms of the KSD assuming the
q
-growth approximation
property of the Stein RKHS

\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}(\mathcal{H}_{K})
(
4
). In
G.3
we show that the term (B) can be made arbitrarily small if we choose
approximation
\tilde{v}_{f}
appropriately. Note that a better approximation
typically increases the complexity (the term C) of the function, measured
in the RKHS norm. We explicitly construct
\tilde{v}_{f}
in Section

G.2
so that we can evaluate
the norm and compare it against the approximation precision. Section

G.3
combines these results
to provide our final results.

G.1
Preparatory lemmas

Lemma G.1

(Tail integral bound by KSD; the Stein equation)
.

Suppose that
{\cal T}_{P}^{m}(\mathcal{H}_{K})

approximates
q
-growth; for
\varepsilon>0,
let
v_{\varepsilon}

be a function such that
{\cal T}_{P}v_{\varepsilon}
is
q
-growth
approximation function, and
r_{\varepsilon}
be the corresponding
radius of the indicator set. Suppose
f\in{\cal F}_{q}.

Then, the solution
v_{f}
to
{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}=f-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f]

in Lemma
3.3
satisfies

\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor 1}\left|{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)\right|\differential Q(x)\leq C\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where

C=2\left(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}c_{m}\zeta_{2}d\right).

Proof.

First, note that the solution
v_{f}
satisfies

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(0)\rvert=\lvert f(x)-f(0)\rvert\leq\bigl(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\bigr)\lVert x\rVert_{2},

where the inequality is due to the
1
-pseudo-Lipschitzness of
f.

By the growth conditions in Assumption
2
,
we have

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(0)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert b(0)\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{f}(0)\rVert_{2}+d\lVert m(0)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\nabla v_{f}(0)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d\right).

These imply

\displaystyle\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor 1}\left|\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)\right|\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor 1}\left|\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(0)\right|\differential Q(x)+\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(0)\rvert\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor 1}\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d\right)\left(\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\differential Q(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d\right)\left(\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}\differential Q+\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d\right)\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where the second inequality follows from
\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q^{\prime}}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}

for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>1
and
q^{\prime}<q;
the third inequality
is due to the
q
-growth approximation property of
\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}.

∎

Lemma G.2

(Tail integral bound by KSD; general functions))
.

Suppose that
{\cal T}_{P}^{m}(\mathcal{H}_{K})

approximates
q
-growth; for
\varepsilon>0,
let
v_{\varepsilon}

be a function such that
{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}
is a
q
-growth
approximation function, and
r_{\varepsilon}
be the corresponding
radius of the indicator set. Let
v\in{\cal C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

be fixed. Assume that
v
satisfies
\lVert v(x)\rVert_{2}\leq C_{0}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}

for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r_{0}
and
C_{0}>0.
For
q_{m}

in Assumption
2
, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r_{0},

assume further
\lVert\nabla v(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}
if

q_{m}=0;
and
\lVert\nabla v(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-2}

if
q_{m}=1.
Then, under Assumption
2
,
we have

\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor r_{0}\lor 1}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)\leq C\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where

C=4\lambda_{b}C_{0}+\lambda_{m}(c_{m}+1)C_{1}\sqrt{d}.

Proof.

By assumption, we have for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r_{\varepsilon}\lor r_{0}

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert b(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert g(x)\rVert_{2}+\sqrt{d}\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\nabla g(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{b}C_{0}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\bigr)+\lambda_{m}C_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigl(c_{m}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1-q_{m}}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\bigr).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor r_{0}\lor 1}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor r_{0}\lor 1}2\lambda_{b}C_{0}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\bigr)\differential Q(x)+\lambda_{m}C_{1}\sqrt{d}\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\lor r_{0}\lor 1}c_{m}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1-q_{m}}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle<\left(4\lambda_{b}C_{0}+(c_{m}+1)\lambda_{m}C_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\left(4\lambda_{b}C_{0}+(c_{m}+1)\lambda_{m}C_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\left(\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}(x)\differential Q+\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(4\lambda_{b}C_{0}+(c_{m}+1)\lambda_{m}C_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where the second inequality follows from
\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q^{\prime}}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}

for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>1
and
q^{\prime}<q;
the third inequality
is due to the
q
-growth approximation property of
{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}.

∎

G.2
Constructive approximation of solutions to the Stein equation

Lemma G.3

(Constructive approximation via convolution)
.

Let
q\geq 1,
and
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Let
w
be the shorthand for
w_{q+\theta-2}(x)=\bigl(\tau^{2}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{(q+\theta-2)/2}

with
\tau>0.
Let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
be a probability
density function satisfying
\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\varphi(x)\differential x<\infty.

Let
v
be a function satisfying the growth Lemma
3.3
.
For
\rho>0
and
r\geq 1,
define

T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{r}^{w}(x)=\rho^{-d}\int\tilde{v}_{r}^{w}(x-y)\varphi(y/\rho)\differential y,\ \text{and}\ \tilde{v}_{r}^{w}(x)=\frac{1_{r,1}(x)}{w(x)}v(x),

where
1_{r,1}\in{\cal C}^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]
defined in
Lemma
K.1
vanishing outside
\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r+1\}

for
r>0.
Then, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wT_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{r}^{w}\bigr)(x)\rvert\leq C\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}r^{q+q_{m}},

where
C
is a constant defined by

C=2^{q}\left\{2\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\lambda_{b}+\lambda_{m}\left(\sqrt{d}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\right)\bigl(c_{m}+1\bigr)\right\}

using constants

\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}=\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\mu_{q}+\bigl(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+3\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\big)\mu_{1},

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\left\{\mu_{q}+(q-1)\left((4+\tau)\tau^{q-3}+\frac{(q-1)^{2}}{\tau^{q}(1\land\tau)}\right)\mu_{1}\right\}\left(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+3\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{=}\quad+\left.\left(\mu_{1}+(q-1)\mu_{q}/(2\tau)\right)\left\{2^{-1}\zeta_{3}+3\zeta_{2}+\left(72+(35+13\sqrt{13})/12+6+3^{4}7^{-1}\right)\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\right\}\right],

and

\mu_{q}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}\left\{1+2\left(1+\lVert x/\tau\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}^{(q-1)/2}\varphi(x)\differential x.

Proof.

By Lemma
K.1
, we have

\displaystyle B_{w}(\rho z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},u\in[0,1]}\frac{w(x)}{w(x-u\rho z)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+2\left(1+\lVert\rho z/\tau\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)\right)^{(q-1)/2}

\displaystyle\leq(1\lor\rho)^{q-1}\left\{1+2\left(1+\lVert z/\tau\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}^{(q-1)/2},

M_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{q-1}{2\tau},

N_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}w(x)^{-1}\left\|\nabla^{2}\log w(x)\right\|_{2}=(q-1)(4+\tau)\tau^{-2},

and

O_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}w(x)^{-1}\left\|\nabla\log w(x)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{(q-1)^{2}}{\tau^{q}(1\land\tau)}.

By Corollaries
I.1
,
I.2

and Lemma
K.1
, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

we have

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}(x)-\tilde{v}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}\{1+(r+1){}^{q-1}\}}{w(x)}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)},

and

\lVert\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}(x)-\nabla\tilde{v}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}\{1+(r+1)^{q-1}\}}{w(x)}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)},

where

\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}=\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\mu_{q}+\bigl(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+3\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\big)\mu_{1},

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\left\{\mu_{q}+(q-1)\left((4+\tau)\tau^{q-3}+\frac{(q-1)^{2}}{\tau^{q}(1\land\tau)}\right)\mu_{1}\right\}\left(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+3\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{=}\quad+\left.\left(\mu_{1}+(q-1)\mu_{q}/(2\tau)\right)\left\{2^{-1}\zeta_{3}+3\zeta_{2}+\left(72+(35+13\sqrt{13})/12+6+3^{4}7^{-1}\right)\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\right\}\right],

and

\mu_{q}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}\left\{1+2\left(1+\lVert x/\tau\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)\right\}^{(q-1)/2}\varphi(x)\differential x.

For
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r,
with
v^{w}(x)=v(x)/w(x),

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wv^{w}\bigr)(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wT_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}\bigr)(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq w(x)\left\{\bigl|\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(v^{w}(x)-T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}(x)\bigr)\bigr|+\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}(x)-\tilde{v}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}(r+1)^{q-1}\left\{2\lambda_{b}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\bigl(1+r\bigr)+\lambda_{m}\left(d\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}+\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\right)\bigl(c_{m}+r{}^{q_{m}+1}\bigr)\right\}.

Thus, if
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r
and
r\geq 1,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wv^{w}\bigr)(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wT_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}^{w}\bigr)(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}2^{q}\left\{2\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\lambda_{b}+\lambda_{m}\left(d\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\right)\bigl(c_{m}+1\bigr)\right\}r^{q+q_{m}}.

∎

Lemma G.4

(Growth of convolution approximation)
.

Let
q\geq 1
and
\theta\in[0,1].

Let
w
be the short hand for
w_{q+\theta-1}(x)=\bigl(\tau^{2}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{(q+\theta-2)/2}.

Let
\varphi
be a probability density. For

\rho>0
and
r\geq 1,
define

T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\rho^{-d}\int\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x-y)\varphi(y/\rho)\differential y,\ \text{where}\ \tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\frac{1_{r,1}(x)}{w(x)}v_{f}(x)

and
1_{r,1}\in{\cal C}^{2}
is a differentiable indicator defined
in Lemma
K.1
,
v_{f}
is in the solution
to the Stein equation
\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v=f-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f]
satisfying
the conditions in Lemma
3.3
, and
v_{f}^{w}(x)=v_{f}(x)/w(x).

Then, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r,

\lVert w(x)T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}\leq A_{0}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\ \text{and}\ \left\|\nabla\Bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\Bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq A_{1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1},

where

A_{0}=2^{2-\theta}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigl(\tau^{2}+1)^{(q-\theta)/2},

and

A_{1}=2^{2-\theta}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(q-\theta)/2}.

Proof.

For
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r,
we have

\displaystyle\lVert w(x)T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq w(x)\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\lVert v_{f}(x)\rVert_{2}}{w(x)}1_{r,1}(x)

\displaystyle\leq w(x)\cdot 2^{2-\theta}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}r^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2-\theta}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigl(\tau^{2}+1)^{(q-\theta)/2}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}.

We also have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{f}(x)\rVert_{2}1_{r,1}(x)}{w(x)}+\frac{1_{r,1}(x)}{w(x)}\lVert\nabla v_{f}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\lVert\nabla 1_{r,1}(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{f}(x)\rVert_{2}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq w(x)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}\bigl(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\bigr)1\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r+1\}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2-\theta}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}r^{1-\theta}

Since each element of
\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}
is continuous
and bounded, we can differentiate under the integral sign
[
Dudley 1999
, Corollary A.5]
;
i.e.,

\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=T_{\varphi,\rho}\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\Bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\Bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq w(x)\left(\left\|\nabla\log w(x)\otimes T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2-\theta}w(x)\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}r^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2-\theta}\left\{\sqrt{d}\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(q-\theta)/2}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}.

∎

Lemma G.5

(Slower gradient growth of convolution approximation)
.

Let
q\geq 1
and
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Let
w
be the shorthand for
w_{q+\theta-2}(x)=\bigl(\tau^{2}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{(q+\theta-2)/2}.

Let
\varphi_{q}

defined as in Lemma
G.7
. For
\rho>0

and
r\geq 1,
define

T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\rho^{-d}\int\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x-y)\varphi_{q}(y/\rho)\differential y,\ \text{where}\ \tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\frac{v_{f}(x)}{w(x)}1_{r,1}(x)

and
1_{r,1}\in{\cal C}^{2}
is a differentiable indicator defined
in Lemma
K.1
,
v_{f}
is in the solution
to the Stein equation
\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v=f-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f]
satisfying
the conditions in Lemma
3.3
, and
v_{f}^{w}(x)=v_{f}(x)/w(x)
.
Then, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>2(r+1),

\left\|\nabla\Bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\Bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{P,d,w}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-2}

where
where
C_{P,d,w}
is a constant given by

\frac{2^{2d+q+3+\theta}\pi{}^{d-1}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{d\cdot\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{1-q}+1)(\tau^{2}+1)^{q/2},

\Gamma
is the Gamma function, and
\mathrm{B}
is the beta function.

Proof.

By
Wendland 2004
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}_{q}(x)^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{q}(2\pi)^{d}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-(d+q)}J_{(d+q)/2}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q}(2\pi)^{d}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-(d+q+1)}\frac{2^{(d+q)+2}}{\pi}

Thus, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r+1,
we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{q}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2(d+q)+2}\pi{}^{d/2-1}\Gamma(d/2)\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-(d+q+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\frac{2^{2(d+q)+2}\pi{}^{d/2-1}\Gamma(d/2)\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}(r+1)^{-(d+q+1-\theta)}}_{C}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}

Since we have

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1\bigr)1\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r+1\}w_{\theta}(x)^{-1},

by Lemma
I.3
, we obtain, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq 2(r+1),

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\theta+1}C\int\lVert\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}\differential x\cdot\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\theta}C\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1)\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}(r+1)^{d+1-\theta}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2d+q+3+\theta}\pi^{d-1}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{d\cdot\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1)(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}.

Also,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{f}(x)\rVert_{2}1_{r,1}(x)}{w(x)}+\frac{1_{r,1}}{w(x)}\lVert\nabla v_{f}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}+\frac{\lVert\nabla 1_{r,1}(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{f}(x)\rVert_{2}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq w(x)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\bigr)1\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r+1\}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\theta}(x)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1\bigr)1\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r+1\}.

Since each element of
\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}
is continuous
and bounded, we can differentiate under the integral sign
[
Dudley 1999
, Corollary A.5]
;
i.e.,
\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=T_{\varphi,\rho}\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x).

Thus, Lemma
I.3
implies

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\theta}C\int\lVert\nabla\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\differential x\cdot\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\theta}C\left\{\sqrt{d}\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\right\}\frac{\bigl(\tau^{1-q}+1\bigr)\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}(\tau^{2}+1)^{\frac{1-\theta}{2}}(r+1)^{d+1-\theta}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2d+q+3+\theta}\pi{}^{d-1}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{d\cdot\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\left\{\sqrt{d}\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\right\}(\tau^{1-q}+1\bigr)(\tau^{2}+1)^{\frac{1-\theta}{2}}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-\theta}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\Bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\Bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=w(x)\left(\left\|\nabla\log w(x)\otimes T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\nabla T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq w(x)\left(\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\left\|T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nabla T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,d,w}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-2}

for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>2(r+1),
where
C_{P,d,w}
is a constant given by

\frac{2^{2d+q+3+\theta}\pi{}^{d-1}\Gamma(q/2+1)^{2}}{d\cdot\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+2^{-1}\zeta_{2}\right\}(\tau^{1-q}+1)(\tau^{2}+1)^{q/2}.

∎

G.3
Proof of
3.2
:
Theorem 3.2

(KSD bound on
d_{{\cal\mathcal{F}}_{q}}
; an informal statement
1
1

1

A precise but less easily interpretable bound is available in
.

)
.

This section elaborates on the informal statement made in Theorem
3.2

in the main text; our proof proceeds as outlined in its proof sketch.

G.3.1
Expectation difference bound for a single function

Lemma G.6

(Expectation difference bound for a single function)
.

Let
q\geq 1
,
\theta\in[0,1]
,
and
w(x)=\bigl(\tau^{2}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{(q+\theta-2)/2}.

Let
K:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

be a matrix-valued kernel. Let
\varphi_{q}
be a probability
density function defined in Lemma
G.7
.
Suppose
\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}(\mathcal{H}_{K})
approximates
q
-growth;
for
\varepsilon>0,
let
v_{\varepsilon}
be a function such that

{\cal T}_{P}^{m}v_{\varepsilon}
is a
q
-growth approximation
function, and
r_{\varepsilon}\geq 1
be the corresponding radius of the
indicator. Then, under Assumptions
2
,

3
,
4
,
for any
f\in{\cal F}_{q},

\rho>0,
and
\varepsilon>0,

we have

\displaystyle\left|\int f\differential P-\int f\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right)+C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{q+q_{m}}+\left\|wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)

where

C=\begin{cases}C_{P,d}^{(1)}+C_{P,d}^{(2)}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=0,\\
C_{P,d}^{(1)}+\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}r_{\varepsilon}+\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=1,\end{cases}

with
C_{P,d}^{(1)},

C_{P,d}^{(2)},

C_{P,d,w}^{(3)},

\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)},
and
\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}
positive
constants independent of
f
(given in the proof); and

T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\rho^{-d}\int\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x-y)\varphi_{q}(y/\rho)\differential y,\ \text{and}\ \tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\frac{1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}(x)}{w(x)}v_{f}(x),

with
1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}\in{\cal C}^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]

defined in Lemma
K.1
vanishing outside

\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}+1\}.

Proof.

For
f\in{\cal F}_{q},
let
v_{f}
be the solution to
the Stein equation
\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}=f-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f]
as in
Lemma
3.3
. For any function
v\in\mathcal{H}_{K}
with
(\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v)_{+}\in L^{1}(Q)
,
we have

\displaystyle\left|\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\bigl(\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v\bigr)\differential Q\right|+\left|\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\bigl(\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v\bigr)\differential Q\right|+\lVert v\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q).

(35)

We split the domain of the integral (the first term) and evaluate as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\int\bigl(\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v\bigr)\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}+\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}}\bigl(\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\bigr)\differential Q(x)\right|

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \leq&\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)+\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)\\
&\quad+\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x).\end{aligned}

(36)

We first deal with the first term of the upper bound (
36
).
By Lemma
G.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)\leq C_{P,d}^{(1)}\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where
C_{P,d}^{(1)}=2(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d).

We next evaluate the third term of (
36
)
by specifying
v
to be an approximation of
v_{f}.
For
\rho>0,

define

T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\rho^{-d}\int\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x-y)\varphi_{q}(y/\rho)\differential y,\ \text{where}\ \tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)=\frac{1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}(x)}{w(x)}v_{f}(x)

and
1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}\in{\cal C}^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]

is a differentiable indicator defined in Lemma
K.1

satisfying
1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}(x)=1
if
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}

and
1_{r_{\varepsilon},1}(x)=0
if
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r_{\varepsilon}+1.

Then, by Lemma
G.3
, the function
wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}

satisfies, for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\bigl(wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}\bigr)(x)\rvert\leq C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{q+q_{m}},

where

C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}=2^{q}\left\{2\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\lambda_{b}+\lambda_{m}\left(d\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(2)}+\frac{q-1}{2\tau}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w}^{(1)}\right)\bigl(c_{m}+1\bigr)\right\}.

Finally, we address the second term of (
36
).
We separately consider the two cases of the growth of
\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}:

q_{m}=0
and
q_{m}=1.
For the linear growth
case
q_{m}=0
, note that by
G.4
,
there exist positive constants
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
such that for

\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r_{\varepsilon}
,

\left\|w(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\right\|_{2}\leq A_{0}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\ \text{and}\ \left\|\nabla\bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq A_{1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}.

Thus, by Lemma
G.2
,
v=wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}

satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)\leq C_{P,d}^{(2)}\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where
C_{P,d}^{(2)}=2\lambda_{b}A_{0}+(c_{m}+1)\lambda_{m}A_{1}\sqrt{d}.

In the case of the quadratic growth
q_{m}=1,
by

G.5
, we have

\left\|\nabla\Bigl(w(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\Bigr)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{P,d,w}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-2}

for some constant
C_{P,d,w}>0
and
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>2(r_{\varepsilon}+1).

We decompose the integral as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{\varepsilon}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)+\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x).

Using
G.4
, the first
integral on the RHS is evaluated as

\displaystyle\int_{r_{\varepsilon}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r_{\varepsilon}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\left(2\lVert b(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert v(x)\rVert_{2}+\sqrt{d}\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\nabla v(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\right)\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{r_{\varepsilon}<\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\left(2\lambda_{b}A_{0}\bigl(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr)\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}+\lambda_{m}A_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigl(c_{m}+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q-1}\right)\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\left(4\lambda_{b}A_{0}+2\lambda_{m}A_{1}\sqrt{d}(1+c_{m})(2r_{\varepsilon}+1)\right)\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q}\differential Q(x)

\displaystyle\leq\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}r_{\varepsilon}\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where the transition from the third line to the fourth follows from

\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)
and
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\geq 1,

and
\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}=6\{2\lambda_{b}A_{0}+\lambda_{m}A_{1}\sqrt{d}(1+c_{m})\}.

The second integral is evaluated using Lemma
G.2

as

\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>2(r_{\varepsilon}+1)}\lvert\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v(x)\rvert\differential Q(x)\leq\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right),

where
\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}=2\lambda_{b}A_{0}+(c_{m}+1)\lambda_{m}C_{P,d,w}\sqrt{d}.

Combining these estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}-\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}\Bigl(wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}\Bigr)\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\bigl(C_{P,d}^{(1)}+C_{P,d}^{(2)}\bigr)\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right)+C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}r_{\varepsilon}^{q}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=0,\\
\left(C_{P,d}^{(1)}+\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}r_{\varepsilon}+\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}\right)\left(\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right)+C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{(q-1)}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{q+1}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=1.\end{cases}

∎

G.3.2
Evaluating the RKHS norm of convolution approximations

We denote by
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
by the respective Lebesgue spaces
of absolutely integrable and square integrable functions; i.e.,
L^{i}=L^{i}(\mathbb{R},\lambda)

for
i\in\{1,2\}
with
\lambda
the
d
-dimensional Lebesgue
measure.
Recall that by
\hat{f}
, we denote the Fourier transform of
f\in L^{1}\cup L^{2}
.

Lemma G.7

(Modified Bochner-Riesz multiplier kernel)
.

Let

\varphi_{q}(x)=\frac{\hat{\Phi}_{q}(x)^{2}}{\lVert\hat{\Phi}_{q}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}}

where

\hat{\Phi}_{q}(x)=2^{q/2}(2\pi)^{d/2}\Gamma(q/2+1)\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-(d+q)/2}J_{(d+q)/2}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr),

and
J_{(d+q)/2}
denotes the Bessel function of the first kind with order
(d+q)/2
.
Then,

\hat{\varphi}_{q}(\omega)\leq\bigl(2\pi\bigr)^{-d/2}\frac{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q/2+1)}{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}1\{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\},

where
\mathrm{B}
is the beta function.

Proof.

By Theorem
J.2
, the function
\hat{\Phi}_{q}

is the Fourier transform of

\Phi_{q}(t)=\begin{cases}\bigl(1-\lVert t\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{q/2}&\text{if}\ \lVert t\rVert_{2}\leq 1,\\
0&\text{if}\ \lVert t\rVert_{2}>1.\end{cases}

By the Plancherel’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\hat{\Phi}_{q}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert\Phi_{q}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq 1}(1-\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q}\differential x

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}\int_{[0,1]}r^{d-1}(1-r^{2})^{q}\differential r

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1).

Thus, by the convolution theorem
[
Wendland 2004
, Theorem 5.16]
,
we obtain for
\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2,

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{q}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\bigl(2\pi\bigr)^{-d/2}\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\pi^{d/2}}\frac{1}{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)}\cdot\Phi_{q}*\Phi_{q}(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(2\pi\bigr)^{-d/2}\frac{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q/2+1)}{\mathrm{B}(d/2,q+1)},

where the inequality is obtained by observing

\displaystyle\Phi_{q}*\Phi_{q}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\int\Phi_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\Phi_{q}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})\differential\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\int\Phi_{q}(\omega^{\prime})\differential\omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}\int_{[0,1]}r^{d-1}(1-r^{2})^{q/2}\differential r

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}\mathrm{B}(d/2+q/2+1).

The claim for the case
\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}>2
holds since
\Phi_{q}*\Phi_{q}(\omega)=0

in the given regime.
∎

Lemma G.8

(RKHS norm evaluation of tilted convolution)
.

Let
w:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to(0,\infty).

Let

k(x,y)=\text{$\Phi_{w}(x-y)+\ell(x,y)$},

where

\Phi_{w}(x,y)=w(x)w(y)\Phi(x-y),

with
\Phi
a positive definite function with a generalized Fourier transform
\hat{\Phi}
, and
\ell
is an arbitrary scalar-valued positive
definite kernel. For
q\geq 1,
let
\varphi_{q}

defined as in Lemma
G.7
. For
\rho>0,

define

T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f(x)=\rho^{-d}\int f(x-y)\varphi_{q}(y/\rho)\differential y,

where
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is in
L^{1}\cap L^{2}.
Then,

\left\|wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}\leq(2\pi)^{-d/4}\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}.

Proof.

According to
Aronszajn 1950
, because

k
is the sum of two kernels, the RKHS norm of
\mathcal{H}_{k}
of a (scalar-valued)
function
h\in\mathcal{H}_{k}
is given by

\lVert h\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}^{2}=\min\{\lVert h_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi_{w}}}^{2}+\lVert h_{2}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}^{2}:h_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Phi_{w}},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{\ell}\text{ s.t. }h=h_{1}+h_{2}\}.

Thus, we can evaluate the norm as

\lVert wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}\leq\lVert wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi_{w}}}=\left\|T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}.

Using the representation of the RKHS norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}

[
Wendland 2004
, Theorem 10.21]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\int\frac{\lvert\widehat{T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f}(\omega)\rvert^{2}}{\hat{\Phi}(\omega)}\differential\omega

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\int\frac{(2\pi)^{d}\lvert\widehat{f}(\omega)\rvert^{2}\lvert\hat{\varphi}_{q}(\rho\omega)\rvert^{2}}{\hat{\Phi}(\omega)}\differential\omega

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{-d/2}\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}\cdot\lVert(2\pi)^{d/2}\hat{f}\hat{\varphi}_{q}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}\cdot\lVert\widehat{T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f}\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

where the second and last equalities are due to the convolution theorem

[
Wendland 2004
, Theorem 5.16]
; the inequality follows
from the bounded support of
\hat{\varphi}_{q}
(Lemma
G.7
).
Since
\varphi_{q}
is in
L^{1}
, and
f\in L^{1}\cap L^{2},

by
[
Stein and Weiss 1971
, Theorem 1.3]
, we have

\left\|T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\right\|_{L^{j}}\leq\lVert f\rVert_{L^{j}}\lVert\varphi_{q}\rVert_{L^{1}}<\infty\ \text{for}\ j\in\{1,2\},

i.e.,
T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\in L^{1}\cap L^{2}.
Thus, the Fourier transform in the first line is well-defined.
Moreover, by the Plancherel theorem
[
Wendland 2004
, Corollary 5.25]
,
the norm of
\widehat{T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f}
is evaluated as

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lVert T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}f\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}\lVert\varphi_{q}\rVert_{L^{1}}=\lVert f\rVert_{L^{2}}.

∎

Lemma G.9

(Norm evaluation of
q
-growth approximator)
.

Consider the same assumptions as
in Lemma
B.3
; define the same symbols.
Let
q\geq 1.
For
\varepsilon>0,
let
\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}

be the function in Lemma
B.3
obtained
using the density
\varphi_{q}
in Lemma
G.7

such that
{\cal T}_{P}\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}(x)\geq\lVert x\rVert^{q}1\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r_{\varepsilon}\}-\varepsilon

with
r_{\varepsilon}\geq 1.
Let
K=k\mathrm{Id}
with

k(x,y)=w_{q-2}(x)w_{q-2}(y)\bigl(w_{\theta}(x)w_{\theta}(y)\Phi(x-y)+k_{\mathrm{lin}}(x,y)\bigr),

for
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Then,

\displaystyle\lVert\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta^{-1}\left(\sqrt{d}+C^{(4)}_{d}r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+1-\theta}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\right)

where

C_{d}^{(4)}=2^{3/2-\theta}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}},

and
\rho>0
is given as in Lemma
B.3
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}
is given as

\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}(x)=2\eta^{-1}w_{q-2}(x)\left(-x-w_{\theta}(x)T_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{0}(x)\right)

where
\tilde{v}_{0}(x)
is a truncated version of
x\mapsto-w_{\theta}(x)^{-1}x
with

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\tilde{v}_{0}(x)\rVert_{2}\leq(r_{\varepsilon}+1)^{1-\theta}

supported in
\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}+1\}.

We have

\displaystyle\lVert(\tilde{v}_{0})_{i}\rVert_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(r_{\varepsilon}+1)^{1-\theta}\sqrt{\lambda\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}+1\}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}(r_{\varepsilon}+1)^{d+2(1-\theta)}},

where
(\tilde{v}_{0})_{i}
is
i
-th component of
\tilde{v}_{0},

and
\lambda
is the
d
-dimensional Lebesgue measure. Also,
for each
i\in\{1,\dots,d\},
we have

\left\|x\mapsto w_{-1}(x)x_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\bar{k}_{\mathrm{lin}}}}=\left\|x_{i}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k_{\mathrm{lin}}}}=1,

where
x_{i}
is the
i
-th coordinate of
x,
and

k_{\mathrm{lin}}(x,y)=\langle x,y\rangle+\tau^{2}.
Note that for an
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued
function
v
, we have

\lVert v\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k\mathrm{Id}}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lVert v_{i}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}^{2}},

where
v_{i}
is the
i
-th component function. Thus,
by Lemma
G.8
and the norm characterization
for sum kernels, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\mathring{v}_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta^{-1}\left(\sqrt{d}+(2\pi)^{-d/4}\sqrt{d}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}(2r_{\varepsilon})^{d+2(1-\theta)}}\cdot\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta^{-1}\left(\sqrt{d}+2^{(3/2-\theta)}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+(1-\theta)}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\right).

∎

Lemma G.10

(Norm of approximate solution to Stein equation)
.

Define symbols as in
G.6
. For
f\in{\cal F}_{q}

and
\rho>0,
we have

\left\|wT_{\varphi_{q},\rho}\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k\mathrm{Id}}}\leq C_{P,d,w}^{(5)}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+1-\theta}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}},

where

C_{P,d,w}^{(5)}=2^{3/2-\theta}\cdot\zeta_{1}\bigl(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1\bigr)\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}.

Proof.

Since
\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}
is supported in
\{\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r_{\varepsilon}+1\}

and satisfies

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\tilde{v}_{f}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}\leq 2^{1-\theta}\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1)(\tau^{2}+1)^{(1-\theta)/2}r_{\varepsilon}^{1-\theta}

the claim follows from Lemma
G.8

and

\lVert v\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k\mathrm{Id}}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lVert v_{i}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}^{2}},

where
v
is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued function, and
v_{i}
is
the
i
-th component function.
∎

G.3.3
Bound for translation-invariant kernels

Theorem G.1

(KSD bound for general translation-invariant kernels)
.

Let
q\geq 1
and
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Let
K=k\mathrm{Id}
with scalar-valued kernel

k(x,y)=w_{q-2}(x)w_{q-2}(y)\bigl(w_{\theta}(x)w_{\theta}(y)\Phi(x-y)+k_{\mathrm{lin}}(x,y)\bigr),

where
\Phi
is assumed to have continuous non-vanishing generalized Fourier transform.
Under Assumptions
2
,
3
,

4
, there exist
r_{\varepsilon}\geq 1

and
\rho_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon,\rho>0,

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,d}(q_{m})r_{\varepsilon}^{q_{m}}\left(2\eta^{-1}\left(\sqrt{d}+C_{d}^{(4)}r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+1-\theta}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\right)\cdot\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)+\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\cdot\rho(1\lor\rho)^{q-1}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{q+q_{m}}+C_{P,d,w}^{(5)}\cdot r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+1-\theta}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\cdot\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q),

where

C_{P,d}(q_{m})=\begin{cases}C_{P,d}^{(1)}+C_{P,d}^{(2)}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=0,\\
C_{P,d}^{(1)}+\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}+\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}&\text{if}\ q_{m}=1;\end{cases}

the constants defining
C_{P,q}(q_{m})
are
given in Theorem
G.6
;

C_{d}^{(4)}
and
C_{P,d,w}^{(5)}
are given in
G.9
and
G.10
, respectively.
In particular, for
a sequence
\{Q_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset{\cal P}
,
the convergence
\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q_{n})\to 0
implies
d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q_{n})\to 0

as
n\to\infty.

Proof.

The conclusion follows from
G.6
using
Lemmas
B.3
,

G.9
,
and
G.10
.
∎

G.3.4
Proof of
3.3
:
Theorem 3.3

(Matérn KSD bound for the linear growth case)
.

See
3.3

Proof.

We use Theorem
G.1
.
Since, according to
Wendland 2004
,
we have
\hat{\Phi}(\omega)=\lvert\Sigma\rvert^{-1}\bigl(1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\omega\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{-d/2-\nu}

with
\lvert\Sigma\rvert
the determinant of
\Sigma
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq\lambda}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq\lambda}\lvert\Sigma\rvert\bigl(1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\omega\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{d/2+\nu}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Sigma\rvert\bigl(1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\lambda^{2}\bigr)^{d/2+\nu}

for any
\lambda>0.
By Lemma
B.3
with
\theta=1
, we
have

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{4}(1\land\delta^{2})(2\land\varepsilon r_{\varepsilon}^{-q})U_{3}^{-1}

for some constant
U_{3}>0.
Then, with
s=d/2+\nu,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 8\eta^{-1}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\left\{1+2^{6}\eta^{-2}\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}U_{3}^{2}(1\lor\delta^{-4})\bigr(1\lor\varepsilon^{-2}r_{\varepsilon}^{2q}\bigr)\right\}^{s/2}.

By Corollary
B.1
, we have
\delta=rW_{r}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}},

and
r_{\varepsilon}=r+\delta=(1+W_{r}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{3}})r

for some constant
r\geq 1
and
W_{r}>0.
Thus,

\displaystyle 1\lor\delta^{-4}

\displaystyle\leq\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-4}\}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-4/3}),

and

r_{\varepsilon}\leq r\bigl(1+W_{r}\bigr)\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3}\bigr).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 8\eta^{-1}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,\Phi,r}\bigl\{(1\lor\varepsilon^{-4/3})(1\lor\varepsilon^{-2}\lor\varepsilon^{-2(q/3+1)}\bigr)\bigr\}^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,\Phi,r}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q+5)/3}\bigr)

with

C_{P,\Phi,r}=\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\left(1\lor 2^{6}\eta^{-2}\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}U_{3}^{2}\right)\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-4}\}^{s/2}\{r(1+rW_{r})\}^{sq}.

Also,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\bigl(1+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\rho^{-2}\bigr)^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\rho^{-s}\bigl(\rho^{2}+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigr)^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\bigl(1+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigr)^{s/2}\bigl(1\lor\rho^{-s}\bigr).

Define the following constants:

\displaystyle A^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\bigl(C_{P,d}^{(1)}+C_{P,d}^{(2)}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2(1+\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+4\lambda_{m}\zeta_{2}c_{m}d)

\displaystyle\hphantom{+}+2\sqrt{d}\bigl(\tau^{2}+1)^{(q-1)/2}\left[2\lambda_{b}\zeta_{1}+\lambda_{m}(c_{m}+1)\left\{\left(\frac{q-1}{\tau}+3\right)\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}+\frac{\zeta_{2}}{2}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle A^{(1)}

\displaystyle=2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}C_{d}^{(4)}\{r(1+W_{r})\}^{d/2}C_{P,\Phi,r}

\displaystyle=2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}}\{r(1+W_{r})\}^{d/2}

\displaystyle\hphantom{=}\quad\cdot\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\left(1\lor 2^{6}\eta^{-2}\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}U_{3}^{2}\right)\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-4}\}^{s/2}\{r(1+rW_{r})\}^{sq},

\displaystyle A^{(2)}

\displaystyle=C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}r\bigl(1+W_{r}\bigr)\bigr\}^{q},

and

\displaystyle A^{(3)}

\displaystyle=C_{P,d.q,w}^{(5)}r^{d/2}\bigl(1+W_{r}\bigr)^{d/2}\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\bigl(1+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigr)^{s/2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\bigl(\tau^{-(q-1)}+1\bigr)\sqrt{\frac{\bigl\{2^{1/2}r\bigl(1+W_{r}\bigr)\bigr\}^{d}\bigl(1+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigr)^{s}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}}.

By Theorem
G.1
,
with
S=\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q),
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{q}}\left|\int\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}v_{f}\differential Q\right|

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\leq 2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}S+A^{(1)}\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-d/6}\bigr)(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q/3+5/3)})S+A^{(0)}\varepsilon\\
&\hphantom{\leq}\quad+A^{(2)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{q-1}\cdot\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-q/3}\bigr)+A^{(3)}\varepsilon^{-d/6}\bigl(1\lor\rho^{-s}\bigr)S.\end{aligned}

(37)

We let
\rho=\varepsilon^{q/3+1}.
Then, the upper bound
becomes

\displaystyle 2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}S+A^{(1)}\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-d/6}\bigr)(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q/3+5/3)}\bigr)S+A^{(0)}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+A^{(2)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{q/3+1})^{q-1}\cdot\bigl(\varepsilon^{q/3}\lor 1\bigr)\varepsilon+A^{(3)}\varepsilon^{-d/6}\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q/3+1)}\bigr)S.

We choose

\varepsilon=S^{\frac{1}{1+t}}\ \text{with}\ t=d/6+s(q+5)/3.

Then, we obtain the final bound

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\{2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}\varepsilon^{t}+A^{(1)}\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-t}\bigr)\varepsilon^{t}+A^{(0)}\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{1+d/6+s(q/3+1)}}}\quad\left.+A^{(2)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{q/3+1})^{q-1}\cdot\bigl(\varepsilon^{q/3}\lor 1\bigr)+A^{(3)}\bigl(\varepsilon^{-s(q/3+1)}\lor 1\bigr)\varepsilon^{t-d/6}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq S^{\frac{1}{1+t}}\left\{2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}S^{\frac{t}{1+t}}+A^{(1)}\bigl(S^{\frac{t}{1+t}}\lor 1\bigr)+A^{(0)}\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\mathcal{S}^{\frac{1}{1+d/6+s(q/3+1)}}}\quad\left.+A^{(2)}(S^{\frac{(q/3+1)(q-1)}{1+t}}\lor 1)\cdot\bigl(S^{\frac{q/3}{1+t}}\lor 1\bigr)+A^{(3)}\bigl(S^{\frac{t-d/6-s(q/3+1)}{1+t}}\lor S^{\frac{t-d/6}{1+t}}\bigr)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\{(2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}+1)A^{(0)}+A^{(1)}+A^{(2)}+A^{(3)}\bigr\}\bigl(S^{\frac{t}{1+t}\lor\frac{q(q/3+1)}{1+t}}\lor 1\bigr)S^{\frac{1}{1+t}}.

Letting
A_{P,d}=(2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}+1)A^{(0)}+A^{(1)}+A^{(2)}+A^{(3)}
concludes the proof.
∎

G.3.5
Proof of
3.4
:
Theorem 3.4

(Matérn KSD bound for the quadratic growth case)
.

See
3.4

Proof.

The proof proceeds as in Theorem
3.3
. By
applying Lemma
B.3
with
\theta=0
, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{4}\left((1\land\delta^{2})\left(2\tilde{C}\land U_{3}^{-1}\varepsilon r_{\varepsilon}^{-(q+1)}\right)\land\frac{4}{\eta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\eta}{4}(1\land\delta^{2})\left(2\tilde{C}\land U_{3}^{-1}\varepsilon r_{\varepsilon}^{-(q+1)}\land\frac{4}{\eta}\right)

for some constants
U_{3},\tilde{C}>0;
we also have.

\displaystyle r_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\left\{2\lor c_{P,d,q,\tau}(1\lor\delta^{-1/d})\right\}(r+\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3d})(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3})

with
\tilde{c}=(2\lor c_{P,d,q,\tau})r(1+W_{r})\bigl(1\lor W_{r}^{-1/d}\bigr).

Then, with
s=d/2+\nu
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Sigma\rvert\left\{1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}2^{6}\eta^{-2}(1\lor\delta^{-4})\bigl(4^{-1}\tilde{C}^{-2}\lor U_{3}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2}r_{\varepsilon}^{2(q+1)}\lor\eta^{2}/2^{4}\bigr)\right\}^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,\Phi,r}\left\{(1\lor\delta^{-4})\left(1\lor\varepsilon^{-2}r_{\varepsilon}^{2(q+1)}\right)\right\}^{s/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,\Phi,r}\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-2s}\}(1\lor\tilde{c}^{s(q+1)})\left(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s\{(q+1)(1+d^{-1})+5\}/3}\right)

where

C_{P,\Phi,r}=\lvert\Sigma\rvert\left[1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}2^{3}\eta^{-2}\left(\tilde{C}^{-2}\lor U_{3}^{2}\lor\eta^{2}/2^{4}\right)\right]^{s/2}.

Define the following constants:

\displaystyle A^{(0)}=\tilde{c}\left(C_{P,d}^{(1)}+\dot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}+\ddot{C}_{P,d}^{(2)}\right),

\displaystyle A^{(1)}=2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}C_{d}^{(4)}\tilde{c}^{d/2+1}C_{P,\Phi,r}\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-2s}\}(1\lor\tilde{c}^{s(q+1)}),

\displaystyle A^{(2)}=C_{P,d,w}^{(3)}\tilde{c}^{q+1}

\displaystyle A^{(3)}=C_{P,d,w}^{(5)}\tilde{c}^{d/2+1}\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\bigl(1+4\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigr)^{s/2}.

Theorem
G.1

implies, with
S=\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q),

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}_{q}}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3})+A^{(1)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-t})(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3})\right\}\cdot S+A^{(0)}\varepsilon(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3})

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+A^{(2)}\rho(1\lor\rho)^{q-1}\cdot(1\lor\varepsilon^{-(q+1)/3})+A^{(3)}\cdot(1\lor\varepsilon^{-d/6})\bigl(1\lor\rho^{-s}\bigr)\cdot S

where

t=\frac{s\{(q+1)(1+d^{-1})+5\}}{3}+\frac{d+2}{6}.

With
\rho=\text{$\varepsilon^{q/3+1}$}
and

\varepsilon=S^{1/(1+t)},
the above upper bound on
d_{{\cal F}_{q}}

is evaluated as follows:

\displaystyle\left\{2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{1/3})\varepsilon^{t}+A^{(1)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{t+1/3})\right\}\varepsilon^{2/3}+A^{(0)}\varepsilon^{2/3}(1\lor\varepsilon^{1/3})

\displaystyle\quad+A^{(2)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{q/3+1})^{q-1}\cdot(\varepsilon^{(q+1)/3}\lor 1)\varepsilon^{2/3}+A^{(3)}\cdot(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q/3+1)-d/6}\bigr)\cdot\varepsilon^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2/3}\left[\left\{2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}(\varepsilon^{t}\lor\varepsilon^{t+1/3})+A^{(1)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{t+1/3})\right\}+A^{(0)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{1/3})\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\varepsilon^{2/3}}\quad\left.+A^{(2)}(1\lor\varepsilon^{(q^{2}+3q-2)/3})+A^{(3)}\cdot(\varepsilon^{t+1/3}\lor\varepsilon^{t+1/3-s(q/3+1)-d/6}\bigr)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}+A^{(1)}+A^{(0)}+A^{(2)}+A^{(3)}\right)(1\lor S^{\frac{t+1/3}{1+t}\lor\frac{(q^{2}+3q-2)}{3(1+t)}})S^{\frac{2}{3}\frac{1}{1+t}}.

Letting
B_{P,d}=2\eta^{-1}\sqrt{d}A^{(0)}+A^{(1)}+A^{(0)}+A^{(2)}+A^{(3)}
concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix H
Bounding Wasserstein distances

In this appendix, we provide a proof for
3.5
.
We begin with a supporting lemma and then provide the proof.

Lemma H.1
.

For
q=(1,\infty)
,
let
P,Q\in{\cal P}_{q}
.
For any coupling
\pi\in\Pi(P,Q)
between
P
and
Q
,
let
(X,Y)
be random variables jointly distributed according to
\pi
,
and
Z=\lVert X-Y\rVert_{2}
.
Suppose we are given a function set
\mathcal{F}
that approximates
q
-growth with respect to
P
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{E}[Z^{q}]\leq 2^{q-1}\left(3r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}\mathbb{E}[Z]+2\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]\right).

(38)

where
f_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{F}
is
q
-growth approximation,
and
r_{\varepsilon}>0
is the corresponding radius.

Proof.

First note that, with
r=r_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert Y\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{Z>r\}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert Y\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{Z>r,\lVert Y\rVert_{2}\leq r\}\bigr]+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\lVert Y\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{Z>r,\lVert Y\rVert_{2}>r\}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq r^{q}Q(Z>r)+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\underbrace{\lVert Y\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{\lVert Y\rVert_{2}>r\}-f_{\varepsilon}(Y)}_{\leq\varepsilon-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]}+f_{\varepsilon}(Y)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq r^{q-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[Z\bigr]+\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}].

where we have used the
q
-growth approximation property and Markov’s inequality to obtain the final line.
The inequality for
\mathbb{E}[\lVert X\rVert_{2}^{q}
is given with
P
substituted for
Q
.
The evaluation above leads to the desired inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z^{q}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Z^{q}1\{Z\leq r_{\varepsilon}\}]+\mathbb{E}[Z^{q}1\{Z>r_{\varepsilon}\}]

\displaystyle\leq r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}\mathbb{E}[Z]+2^{q-1}\left(\mathbb{E}[\lVert X\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{Z>r_{\varepsilon}\}]+\mathbb{E}[\lVert Y\rVert_{2}^{q}1\{Z>r_{\varepsilon}\}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left(3r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}\mathbb{E}[Z]+2\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]\right).

∎

Taking the infimum over all couplings on both sides of (
38
) therefore yields a bound on the
q
-Wasserstein distance
W_{q}
:

\displaystyle W_{q}(P,Q)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left(3r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]\right).

This indicates that using an IPM induced by (possibly a subset of)
\mathcal{F}
,
one can further upper bound
W_{1}(P,Q)
and control

\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]
to obtain an estimate of
W_{q}
.
We will carry out this task below for a Stein RKHS to derive a KSD bound.

Remark 4
.

In fact, one can obtain an inequality similar to (
38
) that holds for any
q>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z^{q}]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q}\left(2^{-1}r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}\mathbb{E}[(Z\land 2r_{\varepsilon})]+2\varepsilon+\mathbb{E}_{Q}[f_{\varepsilon}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[f_{\varepsilon}]\right)

After taking the infimum, we can control the first term in the round brackets by the (scaled) bounded-Lipschitz metric
r_{\varepsilon}^{q}d_{\mathrm{BL}}(P,Q)
(see the proof of
F.1
for the definition).
Therefore, an upper bound is given by any IPM that controls the bounded-Lipschitz metric and is defined by a function class approximating
q
-growth.
For our purpose, we use the slightly tighter (
38
).

H.1
Proof of
3.5
:
Theorem 3.5

(KSD bounds on
W_{q}
)
.

Taking the infimum over all possible couplings in (
38
), with
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}(\mathcal{H}_{K})
, yields

W_{q}(P,Q)^{q}\leq 2^{q-1}\left(3r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon+\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)\right)

(39)

for any
\varepsilon>0
,
where
v_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{H}_{K}
is a function that induces a
q
-growth approximation
f_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{T}_{P}^{m}(v_{\varepsilon})
.

We obtain a bound by choosing an appropriate
\varepsilon
.
In the following, we separately consider the two growth cases of
\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
:
q_{m}\in\{0,1\}
.
Before proceeding, we collect results used to evaluate (
39
).
For our kernel choices, by applying
G.9
with
\theta=1-q_{m}
,

\displaystyle\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta^{-1}\left(\sqrt{d}+2^{1/2+q_{m}}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}r_{\varepsilon}^{d/2+q_{m}}\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\right)

(40)

Here,
v_{\varepsilon}
,
r_{\varepsilon}
,
and
\rho>0
are given as in Lemma
B.3
.
These objects depend on
q_{m}
, and we derive bounds accordingly.
We also make use of
Mackey and Gorham 2016
, which provides the following bound on
W_{1}
:

\displaystyle W_{1}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\{d_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(P,Q)\lor\left(\sqrt{2}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2}d_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}(P,Q)\right)^{1/3}\right\}.

(41)

H.1.1
Linear case
q_{m}=0

Let
s=d/2+\nu
.
From
Section

G.3.4
,
we have

r_{\varepsilon}\leq C_{1}\bigl(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3}\bigr)

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 8\eta^{-1}\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\leq C_{2}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s(q+5)/3}\bigr)

where

\displaystyle C_{1}=r\bigl(1+W_{r}\bigr),

\displaystyle C_{2}=\sqrt{\lvert\Sigma\rvert}\left(1\lor 2^{6}\eta^{-2}\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}U_{3}^{2}\right)\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-4}\}^{s/2}\{r(1+rW_{r})\}^{sq},

with
\eta
,
r
,
U_{3}
and
W_{r}
being constants from
B.3
and
B.1
.

Substituting these estimates into the RHS in (
39
) gives

\displaystyle r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon+\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{q-1}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-(q-1)/3})W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+2\eta^{-1}\left\{\sqrt{d}+2^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}C_{1}^{d/2}C_{2}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-t})\right\}S,

where
t=(1+q/6)d+\nu(q+5)/3
.

As
\mathcal{F}_{1}\subset\mathcal{F}_{q}
, the estimate (
41
) together with

3.3
yields

\displaystyle W_{1}(P,Q)

\displaystyle\leq 3\Bigl(A_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}A_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)(1\lor S^{\frac{2}{3(1+t)}})S^{\frac{1}{3(1+t)}}

(42)

Taking
\varepsilon=S^{\frac{1}{(q+2)(t+1)}}
together with (
42
) results in the following bound on
W_{q}

\displaystyle W_{q}(P,Q)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left[9C_{1}^{q-1}\Bigl(A_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}A_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)(1\lor S^{-\frac{q-1}{3(q+2)(1+t)}})(1\lor S^{\frac{2}{3(1+t)}})S^{\frac{1}{3(1+t)}}\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\left.+2S^{\frac{1}{(q+2)(1+t)}}+2\eta^{-1}\left\{\sqrt{d}+2^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}C_{1}^{d/2}C_{2}(1\lor S^{-\frac{t}{1+t}\frac{1}{q+2}})\right\}S\right]

\displaystyle\leq C^{(0)}_{P,d}(1\lor S^{1-\frac{1}{(q+2)(1+t)}})S^{\frac{1}{(q+2)(1+t)}}

where

C^{(0)}_{P,d}=2^{q}\left\{\frac{9}{2}C_{1}^{q-1}\Bigl(A_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}A_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)+1+\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl(\sqrt{d}+2^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}^{d}2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}C_{2}\Bigr)\right\}

Taking
C_{P,d}(q_{m})=\left(C_{P,d}^{(0)}\right)^{1/q}
completes the proof.
∎

H.1.2
Quadratic case
q_{m}=1

The proof proceeds as in
Section

G.3.5
.
By
B.4
, we have

\displaystyle r_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\left\{2\lor c_{P,d,q,\tau}(1\lor\delta^{-1/d})\right\}(r+\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3d})(1\lor\varepsilon^{-1/3})

with
\tilde{c}=(2\lor c_{P,d,q,\tau})r(1+W_{r})\bigl(1\lor W_{r}^{-1/d}\bigr).

With
s=d/2+\nu
, by applying
B.3
with
\theta=0
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\lVert\omega\rVert_{2}\leq 2\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}}\hat{\Phi}(\omega)^{-1}}\leq C_{3}\left(1\lor\varepsilon^{-s\{(q+1)(1+d^{-1})+5\}/3}\right)

where

C_{3}=C_{P,\Phi,r}\{1\lor(rW_{r})^{-2s}\}(1\lor\tilde{c}^{s(q+1)})

is defined by constants

C_{P,\Phi,r}=\lvert\Sigma\rvert\left[1+\lVert\Sigma^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}2^{3}\eta^{-2}\left(\tilde{C}^{-2}\lor U_{3}^{2}\lor\eta^{2}/2^{4}\right)\right]^{s/2},

U_{3}
, and
\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}_{0}>0
from
B.3
.
With
S=\mathrm{KSD}_{K_{m},P}(Q)
, substituting these estimates into (
39
) and (
40
) provide the following intermedidate bound on
W_{q}
:

\displaystyle W_{q}(P,Q)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left(3r_{\varepsilon}^{q-1}W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon+\lVert v_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}S\right)

(43)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left\{3\tilde{c}^{q-1}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-\tilde{t}})W_{1}(P,Q)+2\varepsilon\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{\eta}\left(\sqrt{d}+2^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}\tilde{c}^{d/2+1}C_{3}(1\lor\varepsilon^{-t})\right)S\right\}

where

t=\frac{s\{(q+1)(1+d^{-1})+5\}}{3}+\frac{d+2}{6},

and
\tilde{t}=(q-1)(1+d^{-1})/3
.
Moreover, the estimate (
41
) together with

3.4
yields

\begin{aligned} W_{1}(P,Q)&\leq 3\Bigl(B_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}B_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)(1\lor S^{\frac{4}{9(1+t)}})S^{\frac{2}{9(1+t)}}\end{aligned}.

(44)

Combining (
43
) and (
44
) provides the final bound:

\displaystyle W_{q}(P,Q)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{q-1}\left\{9\tilde{c}^{q-1}\Bigl(B_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}B_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)(1\lor S^{\frac{6(1+\tilde{t})-1}{9(1+t)(1+\tilde{t})}})S^{\frac{2}{9(1+t)}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\tilde{t}}}\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq 2^{q-1}}\left.2S^{\frac{2}{9(1+t)}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\tilde{t}}}+\frac{2}{\eta}\Bigl(\sqrt{d}+2^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}\tilde{c}^{d/2+1}C_{3}(1\lor S^{1-\frac{2}{9(1+t)}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\tilde{t}}})\Bigr)S^{\frac{2}{9(1+t)}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\tilde{t}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{P,d}^{(1)}(1\lor S^{1-\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}\frac{2}{3(1+t)}\cdot})S^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}\frac{2}{3}\frac{1}{1+t}\cdot},

where

\displaystyle C_{P,d}^{(1)}=2^{q}\left\{\frac{9}{2}\tilde{c}^{q-1}\Bigl(B_{P,d}\lor(\sqrt{2}B_{P,d}\mathbb{E}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}]^{2})^{1/3}\Bigr)+1+\frac{1}{\eta}\left(\sqrt{d}+2^{3/2}\sqrt{\frac{2^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2)}}\tilde{c}^{d/2+1}C_{3}\right)\right\},

and

\tilde{q}=(1+d^{-1})q+(2-d^{-1}).

Taking
C_{P,d}(q_{m})=\left(C_{P,d}^{(1)}\right)^{1/q}
completes the proof.
∎

Appendix I
Results concerning approximation via convolution

This section collects results concerning convolution. Specifically,
we consider the convolution operator
T_{\varphi,\rho}
defined as

T_{\varphi,\rho}f(x)=\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int f(x-y)\varphi(y/\rho)\differential y,

where
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}},\lambda),

\varphi\in L^{1}\bigl(\lambda\bigr)

with
\int\varphi(x)\differential x=1,

\lambda
the
d
-dimensional
Lebesgue measure, and
\rho>0.

Lemma I.1

(Approximation error)
.

Let
g\in{\cal C}^{1}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

For
r\in(0,\infty],
let
\iota_{r}\in{\cal C}^{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]

such that
\iota_{r}(x)=0
for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r.
Let
\tilde{g}_{r}=\iota_{r}g.

For
w\in{\cal C}^{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to(0,\infty),
define

B_{w}(z)=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},u\in[0,1]}w(x)/w(x-uz)\ \text{and}\ M_{w}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}.

Let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty]
be a function such that

\int\varphi(x)\differential x=1,

\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}B_{w}(\rho x)\varphi(x)\differential x<\infty,

and
\mu_{\varphi}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}\varphi(x)\differential x.
With fixed
\rho>0,

define the convolution

T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)=\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x-z)\varphi(z/\rho)\differential z,\ \text{where}\ \tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\coloneqq\frac{\tilde{g}_{r}(x)}{w(x)}.

Then, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
we have

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left\{M_{w}\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\lVert g(y)\rVert_{2}+\mu_{\varphi}\Bigl(\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|\nabla g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+C_{r,1}\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{2}}\Bigr)\right\},

where
C_{r,1}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{2}.

Proof.

Let
Z
be a random variable whose law is a distribution having
\varphi

as the density with respect to the Lebesgue measure. By the definition
of
T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w},
we have

T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)\left\{\frac{1}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{1}{w(x)}\right\}+\frac{\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\tilde{g}_{r}(x)}{w(x)}\right].

To bound each quantity inside the expectation, we derive their norm
estimates as follows. First, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-t\rho Z)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert Z\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\rho\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\left(\max_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|\nabla g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\max_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{2}}\cdot\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(y)\rVert_{2}\right),

where the first line is due to the mean value theorem with
t\in(0,1).

Similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{1}{w(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\rho\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\left\|\nabla_{x}\frac{1}{w(x-t\rho Z)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho\lVert Z\rVert_{2}}{w(x)}\frac{w(x)}{w(x-t\rho Z)}\left\|\nabla_{x}\log w(x-t\rho Z)\right\|_{2}

(45)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho\left\|Z\right\|_{2}}{w(x)}B_{w}(\rho Z)M_{w},

where
t\in(0,1).
Combining these evaluations yields

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x-\rho Z)\rVert_{2}\left|\frac{1}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{1}{w(x)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+\frac{1}{w(x)}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x-\rho Z)-\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left[M_{w}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\Bigl[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\Bigr]\max_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\lVert g(y)\rVert\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}}\left.\quad+\mathbb{E}_{Z}\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\left(\max_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|\nabla g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\max_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{2}}\cdot\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(y)\rVert_{2}\right)\right]

∎

Corollary I.1

(Approximation error with respect to solutions of Stein equations)
.

Let
v:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}

be a function satisfying the growth conditions in Lemma
3.3
.
Define symbols as in Lemma
I.1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{v}_{r}^{w}(x)-\tilde{v}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho(1+r^{q-1})}{w(x)}\left[\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}M_{w}\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}+\mu_{\varphi}\bigl(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}C_{r,1}\big)\right]

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Lemma I.2

(Derivative approximation error)
.

Let
g\in{\cal C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

For
r\in(0,\infty],
let
\iota_{r}\in{\cal C}^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]

such that
\iota_{r}(x)=0
for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}>r.
Let
\tilde{g}_{r}=\iota_{r}g.

For
w\in{\cal C}^{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to(0,\infty),
define the following quantities:

B_{w}(z)=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},u\in[0,1]}w(x)/w(x-uz)

M_{w}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2},\ N_{w}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\lVert\nabla^{2}\log w(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}}{w(x)}

and

O_{w}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}^{2}}{w(x)}.

Let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty]
be a function such that

\int\varphi(x)\differential x=1,

\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}B_{w}(\rho x)\varphi(x)\differential x<\infty,

and
\mu_{\varphi}=\int\lVert x\rVert_{2}\varphi(x)\differential x.
With fixed
\rho>0,

define the convolution

T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)=\frac{1}{\rho^{d}}\int\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x-z)\varphi(z/\rho)\differential z,\ \text{where}\ \tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\coloneqq\frac{\tilde{g}_{r}(x)}{w(x)}.

Assume
\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)=T_{\varphi,\rho}\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)

for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.
Then, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

we have

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left[\left\{M_{w}\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}+\left(N_{w}+O_{w}\right)\mu_{\varphi}\right\}\left(\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|\nabla g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+C_{r,1}\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|g(y)\right\|_{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}}\quad\left.+(\mu_{\varphi}+M_{w}\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w})\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left(\lVert\nabla^{2}g(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+2C_{r,1}\lVert\nabla g(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+C_{r,2}\lVert g(y)\rVert_{2}\right)\right],

where
C_{r,1}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{2},

C_{r,2}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.

Proof.

First, note that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\lVert\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\Bigl[\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\Bigr]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\\
&\leq\underbrace{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\frac{\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)}{w(x)}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{\mathrm{(a)}}\\
&\hphantom{\leq\quad}+\underbrace{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\frac{\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}\otimes\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\frac{\nabla\log w(x)}{w(x)}\otimes\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{\mathrm{(b)}},\end{aligned}

(46)

In the first line, we have exchanged the gradient and the expectation
operation. We evaluate each term below.

The term (a) is evaluated as

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\frac{\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)}{w(x)}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)\left(\frac{1}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{1}{w(x)}\right)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{(\mathrm{a1})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{w(x)}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{\mathrm{(a2)}}.

(47)

The term (a1) is bounded as

\displaystyle\mathrm{(a1)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\left|\frac{1}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{1}{w(x)}\right|\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}M_{w}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\right]\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

The term (a2) is evaluated as follows. By the mean value theorem,
for some
t\in(0,1),

\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)=-\rho\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(x-t\rho Z)\bar{\times}_{1}Z,

where
(A\times_{1}v)_{ij}=\sum_{k}A_{kij}v_{k}
for
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}\times d_{3}}

and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}.
This implies

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\rho\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(x-t\rho Z)\bar{\times}_{1}Z\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\rho\sup_{\lVert u_{2}\rVert_{2}=1,\lVert u_{3}\rVert_{2}=1}\left|\langle\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(x-t\rho Z),Z\otimes u_{2}\otimes u_{2}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\rho\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.

Thus, the operator norm in the term (a2) is bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\bigr]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigl[\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\rho\mathbb{E}_{Z}\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.

Multiplying by
w(x)^{-1}
completes the evaluation of (a2).

Similarly, we can evaluate the term (b) in (
46
).
We again apply the mean value theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left\|\frac{\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{\nabla\log w(x)}{w(x)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\right]\cdot\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\left\|\frac{\nabla^{2}\log w(y)}{w(y)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{1}{w(y)}\left\|\nabla\log w(y)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{(b)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\frac{\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}\otimes\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\frac{\nabla\log w(x)}{w(x)}\otimes\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{w(x)}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\frac{w(x)\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}\otimes\bigl(\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\bigr)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+\left\|\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\left(\frac{\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{\nabla\log w(x)}{w(x)}\right)\otimes\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{w(x)}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left\|\frac{w(x)\bigl(\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)\bigr)}{w(x-\rho Z)}\right\|_{2}\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x-\rho Z)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left\|\frac{\nabla\log w(t-\rho Z)}{w(x-\rho Z)}-\frac{\nabla\log w(x)}{w(x)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho M_{w}}{w(x)}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\right]\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq}\quad+\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left(N_{w}+O_{w}\right)\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\right]\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left\{M_{w}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\right]\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\left(N_{w}+O_{w}\right)\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\right]\lVert\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}.

Since the operator norm of
\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\tilde{g}_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\\
&\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\iota_{r}(x)\lVert\nabla^{2}g(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+2\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{2}\lVert\nabla g(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\lVert\nabla^{2}\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert g(x)\rVert_{2}\right).\end{aligned}

(48)

Combining the above evaluations, we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}\left[\left\{M_{w}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\left\|Z\right\|_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\right]+\left(N_{w}+O_{w}\right)\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}\right]\right\}\cdot A_{r,1}\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{\leq\frac{\rho}{w(x)}}\quad\left.+\left(\mathbb{E}_{Z}\lVert Z\rVert_{2}+M_{w}\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left[\lVert Z\rVert_{2}B_{w}(\rho Z)\right]\right)A_{r,1}\right],

where

A_{r,1}=\left(\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|\nabla g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+C_{r,1}\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left\|g(y)\right\|_{\mathrm{2}}\right),

and

A_{r,2}=\sup_{\lVert y\rVert_{2}\leq r}\left(\lVert\nabla^{2}g(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+2C_{r,1}\lVert\nabla g(y)\rVert_{2}+C_{r,2}\lVert g(y)\rVert_{2}\right)

with
C_{r,1}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{2}
and

C_{r,2}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{2}\iota_{r}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
.
∎

Corollary I.2

(Derivative approximation error with respect to solutions of Stein equations)
.

Define symbols as in
Lemma
I.1
. Let
v:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}

be a function satisfying the growth conditions in Lemma
3.3
.
For each fixed
\rho>0,
we have

\lVert\nabla T_{\varphi,\rho}\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)-\nabla\tilde{g}_{r}^{w}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{\rho(1+r^{q-1})}{w(x)}\tilde{u}_{P,d,w,\rho}^{(2)},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{P,d,w,\rho}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left[M_{w}\left\{\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}+\left(N_{w}+O_{w}\right)\mu_{\varphi}\right\}\left(2^{-1}\zeta_{2}+C_{r,1}\zeta_{1}\sqrt{d}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hphantom{=}\quad+\left.\left(\mu_{\varphi}+M_{w}\mu_{\varphi,\rho,w}\right)\left(2^{-1}\zeta_{3}+C_{r,1}\zeta_{2}+C_{r,2}\sqrt{d}\zeta_{1}\right)\right].

Lemma I.3

(Growth of convolution functions)
.

Let
\lVert\cdot\rVert
be an arbitrary
norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}.
Let
{\cal S}
be a separable Banach space
with norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{{\cal S}}.
Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{S}

be a function supported in
\{\lVert x\rVert\leq r_{f}\}
for some
r_{f}>0.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a real-valued function satisfying

\displaystyle\lvert g(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\Delta_{g}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert\bigr),

for
\lVert x\rVert\geq r_{g},
some positive constants
C_{g},

r_{g},

and an non-increasing function
\Delta_{g}.
Then, the convolution

h(x)=\int f(y)g(x-y)\differential y

satisfies

\displaystyle\lVert h(x)\rVert_{{\cal S}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\left(\int\lVert f(y)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\differential y\right)\cdot\Delta_{g}(2^{-1}\lVert x\rVert)

for
\lVert x\rVert\geq 2(r_{f}\lor r_{g}),
where
h
is defined as the
Bochner integral. In particular, if
\lVert f\rVert_{\infty}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert f(x)\rVert_{{\cal S}}<\infty,

\lVert h(x)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\leq C_{g}\lVert f\rVert_{\infty}\lambda(\{\lVert x\rVert\leq r_{f}\})\Delta_{g}(2^{-1}\lVert x\rVert),

where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure.

Proof.

Let
r=(r_{f}\lor r_{g}).
For
\lVert y\rVert\leq r
and
\lVert x\rVert>2r\geq 2\lVert y\rVert,

we have

\displaystyle\lVert x-y\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\lVert x\rVert-\lVert y\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lVert x\rVert}{2}>r.

Thus, under these conditions, we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert h(x)\rVert_{{\cal S}}

\displaystyle\leq\int\lvert g(x-y)\rvert\lVert f(y)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\differential y=\int_{\lVert y\rVert\leq r}\lvert g(x-y)\rvert\lVert f(y)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\differential y

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\int_{\lVert y\rVert\leq r}\Delta_{g}(\lVert x-y\rVert)\lVert f(y)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\differential y

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}\Delta_{g}(2^{-1}\lVert x\rVert)\int_{\lVert y\rVert\leq r}\lVert f(y)\rVert_{{\cal S}}\differential y.

∎

Appendix J
Known results

Theorem J.1

(Finite Stein factors from Wasserstein decay,
Erdogdu et al. 2018
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
2
,

3
, and
4

hold. Assume
f
is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
q
with
at most degree-
q
polynomial growth of its
i
-th derivatives
for
i=2,3,4
. Then, the solution
u_{f}
to the equation (
13
)
is pseudo-Lipschitz of order
q
with constant
\zeta_{1},

and has
i
th order derivative with degree-
q
polynomial
growth for
i=2,3,4:

\lVert\nabla^{i}u_{f}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\zeta_{i}(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{q})\ \text{for }i\in\{2,3,4\},\ \text{and }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

The constants
\zeta_{i}
(called Stein factors) are given as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}

\displaystyle=\tau_{i}+\xi_{i}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{1}(t)\omega_{q_{m}+1}(t+i-2)\differential t\ \text{for }i=1,2,3,4,

where

\omega_{q_{m}+1}(t)=1+4\rho_{1}(t)^{1-1/(q_{m}+1)}\rho_{1}(0)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\alpha}_{q_{m}+1}^{q}}\{(1\vee\tilde{\rho}_{q_{m}+1}(t))8\lambda_{m}q+3(q_{m}+1)\beta\}^{q}\right],

with
\tilde{\alpha}_{1}=\alpha,

\tilde{\alpha}_{2}=\inf_{t\geq 0}[\alpha-4q\lambda_{m}(1\vee\tilde{\rho}_{2}(t)]_{+},

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=0,\ \tau_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{PL},q}\tilde{\pi}(f)_{2:i,q}\tilde{\nu}_{1:q}(\sigma)\kappa_{q_{m}}(6q)\ \text{for }i=2,3,4,

\displaystyle\xi_{1}=\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{PL},q},

\displaystyle\xi_{i}=\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{PL},q}\tilde{\nu}_{1:i}(b)\tilde{\nu}_{0:i-2}(\sigma^{-1})\rho_{1}(0)\omega_{q_{m}+1}(1)\kappa_{q_{m}+1}(6q)^{i-1}\ \text{for }i=2,3,4,

where
\tilde{\pi}(f)_{i,q}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{i}f(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}/(1+\lVert x\rVert^{q}),

\tilde{\pi}(f)_{a:b,q}\coloneqq\max_{i=a,\dots,b}\tilde{\pi}(f)_{i,q},

\tilde{\nu}_{a:b}(g)
is a constant whose precise form is given
in the proof of
Erdogdu et al. 2018
, and

\kappa_{q_{m}+1}(q)=2+\frac{2\beta}{\alpha}+\frac{q\lambda_{m}}{\alpha}+\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{q_{m}+1}}{\alpha}\left(\frac{4q\lambda_{m}+6(q_{m}+1)\beta}{2r\tilde{\alpha}_{q_{m}+1}}\right).
