Theorem J.2

(
Stein and Weiss 1971
)
.

If
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]

is the function defined by

\Phi(t)=\begin{cases}\bigl(1-\lVert t\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{\delta}&\lVert t\rVert_{2}\leq 1\\
0&\lVert t\rVert_{2}>1,\end{cases}

where
\delta>0,
then

\hat{\Phi}(x)=2^{\delta}(2\pi)^{d/2}\Gamma(\delta+1)\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{-(d/2)-\delta}J_{d/2+\delta}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr),

with
J_{d/2+\delta}
the Bessel function of the first kind
with order
d/2+\delta.

Remark
.

The above result differs from the cited result
[
Stein and Weiss 1971
, Theorem 4.15]

in the definition of the Fourier transform. We use

\hat{f}(\omega)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\int f(x)e^{-i\langle x,\omega\rangle}\differential x

whereas
Stein and Weiss 1971
uses

\tilde{f}(\omega)=\int f(x)e^{-i2\pi\langle x,\omega\rangle}\differential x.

We have
\tilde{f}(\omega)=\hat{f}(2\pi\omega).
The same
applies to the inverse transform.

Appendix K
Miscellaneous results

Lemma K.1

(Constants for multiquadratic functions)
.

Let
w(x)=\Bigl(a+b\lVert x-\mu\rVert_{2}^{2}\Bigr)^{q}

with
a>0,

b>0,

q\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then,

\displaystyle B_{w}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},u\in[0,1]}\frac{w(x)}{w(x-uz)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{1+2\left(1+\frac{b\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2}}{a}\right)\right\}^{\lvert q\rvert},

M_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\rVert_{2}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{2b\lvert q\rvert\lVert x-\mu\rVert_{2}}{a+b\lVert x-\mu\rVert_{2}^{2}}\leq\lvert q\rvert\sqrt{\frac{b}{a}},

and

N_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lVert\frac{\nabla^{2}\log w(x)}{w(x)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 6\lvert q\rvert\frac{b}{a}.

If
q\geq-1
, then

\displaystyle O_{w}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left\lVert\nabla\log w(x)\right\rVert^{2}_{2}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq(2bq)^{2}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}}{\bigl(a+b\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{2+q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4bq^{2}}{a^{q+1}}\left\{\frac{(1+q)^{1+q}}{(2+q)^{2+q}}\lor 1\{q=-1\}\right\}.

Proof.

Suppose
q\geq 0
. With
\bar{x}=x-\mu,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{w(x+uz)}{w(x)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{a+b\lVert\bar{x}+uz\rVert_{2}^{2}}{a+b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right)^{q}\leq\left(1+\frac{\lVert\bar{x}+uz\rVert_{2}^{2}}{(a/b)+\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+2\frac{\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2}}{(a/b)+\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{q}}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{1+2\left(1+\frac{b\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2}}{a}\right)\right\}^{q}.

The first claim follows from the observation

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},u\in[0,1]}\frac{w(x)}{w(x-uz)}\leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{w(x+uz)}{w(x)}.

The derivation above applies to
q<0
(replace
q
with
\lvert q\rvert
).
The second and final claims can be checked easily.
The third claim follows from

\displaystyle N_{w}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{\nabla^{2}\log w(x)}{w(x)}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{w(x)}\left\|\left(\frac{\nabla^{2}w(x)}{w(x)}-\frac{\nabla w(x)\otimes\nabla w(x)}{w(x)^{2}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}8b^{2}\lvert q\rvert\left\|\frac{\bar{x}\otimes\bar{x}}{\bigl(a+b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+2\lvert q\rvert b\left\|\frac{I}{a+b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}8\lvert q\rvert\frac{b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{\bigl(a+b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{2}}+2\lvert q\rvert b\frac{1}{a+b\lVert\bar{x}\rVert_{2}^{2}}\leq 6\lvert q\rvert\frac{b}{a}

∎

Lemma K.2

(Derivative constants of a multi-quadratic function)
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{R}
.
The multiquadratic function
w(x)=(1+\lVert x\rVert^{2}_{2})^{q}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla w(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lvert q\rvert w(x),\lVert\nabla^{2}w(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lvert q(q+1)\rvert w(x),\ \text{and}

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{3}w(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\lvert q(q-1)(q+4)\rvert w(x)

Proof.

The results follow from the following expressions:

\displaystyle\nabla w(x)

\displaystyle=2q(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q-1}x,

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}w(x)

\displaystyle=4q(q-1)(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q-2}x\otimes x+2q(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q-1}\mathrm{Id},

\displaystyle\nabla^{3}w(x)

\displaystyle=8q(q-1)(q-2)(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q-3}x\otimes x\otimes x

\displaystyle\quad+4q(q-1)(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})^{q-2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(e_{i}\otimes e_{i}\otimes x+e_{i}\otimes x\otimes e_{i}+x\otimes e_{i}\otimes e_{i}\right).

∎

Lemma K.3

(
\mathcal{C}^{2}
-cutoff function)
.

Let
f(t)=t^{3}1_{(0,\infty)}(t).
Let

\text{$h(t)$}=\frac{f\bigl(r+\delta-t\bigr)}{f\bigl(r+\delta-t\bigr)+f\bigl(t-r\bigr)}.

Then,
h
is twice continuously differentiable; we have
h(t)=0

for
t\geq r+\delta,

h(t)=1
for
t\leq r,
and
0<h(t)<1
otherwise.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\left|\derivative{h}{t}\right|(t)\leq 3\delta^{-1},

\displaystyle\left|\derivative[2]{h}{t}\right|(t)\leq\left(72+\frac{35+13\sqrt{13}}{12}\right)\delta^{-1}+2^{3+2/3}\delta^{-2}.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the twice continous differentiablity
of
f,
and the second claim holds by the definition of
f.
To
evaluate the derivatives, we consider the interval
(r,r+\delta),

as
h
is constant outside this interval. By reparametrizing
t=\theta(1+\theta)^{-1}\delta+r

with
\theta\in(0,\infty),

h(\theta)=\frac{1}{1+\theta^{3}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left|\derivative{h}{t}\right|=\left|\derivative{h}{\theta}\derivative{\theta(t)}{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta^{-1}\frac{\theta^{2}(1+\theta)^{2}}{(1+\theta^{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta^{-1},

and

\displaystyle\left|\derivative[2]{h}{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\derivative[2]{h}{\theta}\derivative{\theta(t)}{t}\right|+\left|\derivative{h}{\theta}\derivative[2]{\theta(t)}{t}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\left(\frac{18\theta^{4}}{(1+\theta^{3})^{3}}+\frac{6\theta}{(1+\theta^{3})^{2}}\right)(1+\theta)^{2}+\frac{3\theta^{2}}{(1+\theta^{3})^{2}}\cdot\frac{2(1+\theta)^{3}}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(18\cdot 4+6\cdot\frac{35+13\sqrt{13}}{72}\right)\delta^{-1}+2^{3+2/3}\delta^{-2}

∎

Corollary K.1

(
\mathcal{C}^{2}
-cutoff function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
)
.

f(t)=t^{3}1_{(0,\infty)}(t).

Let
1_{r,\delta}(x)=h\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr)
be a
\mathcal{C}^{2}
-function
defined by

\text{$h(t)$}=\frac{f\bigl(r+\delta-t\bigr)}{f\bigl(r+\delta-t\bigr)+f\bigl(t-r\bigr)}.

Then, we have
1_{r,\delta}(x)=1
for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\leq r,

0<1_{r,\delta}(x)<1

for
r<\lVert x\rVert_{2}<r+\delta,
and
1_{r,\delta}(x)=0
for
\lVert x\rVert_{2}\geq r+\delta;

the gradient
\nabla 1_{r,\delta}(x)
vanishes outside
\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:r<\lVert x\rVert_{2}<r+\delta\}.

Furthermore, we can uniformly bound the derivative norms
\lVert\nabla_{x}1_{r,\delta}(x)\rVert_{2}

and
\lVert\nabla_{x}1_{r,\delta}(x)\rVert_{2}
by constants depending
only on
\delta.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{x}1_{r,\delta}(\lVert x\rVert_{2})\rVert_{2}=\left\|\frac{x}{\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\left.\derivative{h}{t}\right|_{t=\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\right\|_{2}\leq 3\delta^{-1},

and

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{x}^{2}1_{r,\delta}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle<\left|\derivative[2]{h}{t}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr)\right|+\max_{r\leq t\leq r+\delta}\frac{2}{t}\left|\derivative{h}{t}\bigl(\lVert x\rVert_{2}\bigr)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(72+\frac{35+13\sqrt{13}}{12}+\frac{6}{r}\right)\delta^{-1}+2^{3+2/3}\delta^{-2}

Proof.

We check the statement about the second derivatives. For
r<\lVert x\rVert_{2}<r+\delta,

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla^{2}1_{r,\delta}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla\left(h^{\prime}(\lVert x\rVert)\frac{x}{\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla h^{\prime}(\lVert x\rVert_{2})\otimes\frac{x}{\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{\lvert h^{\prime}(\lVert x\rVert)\rvert}{\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\left\|\mathrm{Id}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\frac{\lvert h^{\prime}(\lVert x\rVert)\rvert}{\lVert x\rVert^{3}}\lVert x\otimes x\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert h^{\prime\prime}(\lVert x\rVert_{2})\rvert+\frac{2}{\lVert x\rVert_{2}}\lvert h^{\prime}(\lVert x\rVert)\rvert.

The rest of the proof follows from Lemma
K.3
.
∎

Lemma K.4

(Student’s
t
-distributions are dissipative)
.

For
\nu>2,
let the density of the standard multivariate

t
-distribution be

p(x)=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\nu+d}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\nu}{2}\right)\nu^{\frac{d}{2}}\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}}\left(1+\frac{\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}}{\nu}\right)^{-\frac{\nu+d}{2}}.

Let
m(x)=(1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})\mathrm{Id}.
Then, Assumption
2

holds with
\lambda_{b}=(\nu+d-2)/(2\nu)
and
\lambda_{m}=\nu^{-1},

and Assumption
1
with
\alpha=1-2\nu^{-1},

\beta_{1}=0,
and
\beta_{0}=d.
Moreover, the diffusion
(
12
) satisfies the dissipativity condition in
Proposition
3.4
with
q=1

and
\sigma(x)=\sqrt{1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}}\mathrm{Id}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\nabla\log p(x)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\nu+d}{\nu}\frac{x}{1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}},\ \text{and}

\displaystyle 2b(x)

\displaystyle=m(x)\nabla\log p(x)+\langle\nabla,m(x)\rangle=-\frac{\nu+d-2}{\nu}x.

Thus,

\displaystyle\lVert b(x)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu+d-2}{2\nu}\lVert x\rVert\ \text{and}\

\displaystyle\lVert m(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert^{2}=\nu^{-1}(\nu+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}).

The dissipativity holds since

\displaystyle 2\langle b(x),x\rangle+\lVert\sigma(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu+d-2}{\nu}\lVert x\rVert^{2}+d(1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle=-(1-2\nu^{-1})\lVert x\rVert^{2}+d.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 2\langle b(x)-b(y),x-y\rangle+\lVert\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\lVert\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\nu+d-2}{\nu}\lVert x-y\rVert_{2}^{2}+(d-1)\left(\sqrt{1+\nu^{-1}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{2}}-\sqrt{1+\nu^{-1}\lVert y\rVert_{2}^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\nu+d-2}{\nu}\lVert x-y\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{d-1}{\nu}\lVert x-y\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left(1-\frac{1}{\nu}\right)\lVert x-y\rVert_{2}^{2},

showing the uniform dissipativity.
∎

Lemma K.5

(Derivative norm estimates of Gaussian smoothing; an extension of
Mackey and Gorham 2016
, Lemma 2.2
)
.

Let
G
be a
d
-dimensional standard normal vector, and
s>0
.
Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a pseudo-Lipschitz function of order
s
with
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{PL},s}\leq 1
.
Then, the Gaussian convolution
f_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}[f(x+tG)]
satisfies, for each
t>0
,

\displaystyle M_{1,s}(f_{t})\leq 1+C_{t,s},\ M_{2,s}(f_{t})\leq\frac{1}{t}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}+C_{t,s}\right),\ M_{3,s}(f_{t})\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{t^{2}}(1+C_{t,s})

where
M_{i,s}(f_{t})=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lVert\nabla^{i}f_{t}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}/(1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s})
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
,
and

C_{t,}=1\{s>0\}\cdot(1\lor 2^{s-1})\Bigl(1+t^{s}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{G}[\lVert G\rVert_{2}^{2s}]}\Bigr).

Proof.

We address
s>0
, as the case
s=0
is treated in
Mackey and Gorham 2016
. The following proof closely follow theirs.

Since a pseudo-Lipschitz function is locally Lipschitz, by Rademacher’s theorem, the gradient of
f
exists almost everywhere and satisfies

\lVert\nabla f(x)\rVert_{2}\leq 1+\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}.

(49)

Moreover, from the proof of
Mackey and Gorham 2016
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla f_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int\nabla f(x+tz)\phi(z)\differential z=\frac{1}{t}\int zf(x+tz)\phi(z)\differential z

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\int\bigl(\nabla f(x+tz)\otimes z\bigr)\phi(z)\differential z=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int f(x+tz)(z\otimes z-\mathrm{Id})\phi(z)\differential z

\displaystyle\nabla^{3}f_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\int\nabla f(x+tz)\otimes(z\otimes z-\mathrm{Id})\phi(z)\differential z,

where
\phi(z)
is the density of the standard normal distribution.

From the first equality and the estimate (
49
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla f_{t}(x)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\int\bigl(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}+c_{s}t^{s}\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{s}\bigr)\phi(z)\differential z,

where
c_{s}=1\lor 2^{s-1}
.
From the second expression,
for any
v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\lVert v_{1}\rVert=\lVert v_{2}\rVert=1
,

\displaystyle\lvert\langle\nabla^{2}f_{t}(x),v_{1}\otimes v_{2}\rangle\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\left\lvert\int\langle\nabla f(x+tz),v_{1}\rangle\langle z,v_{2}\rangle\phi(z)\differential z\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\int\lVert\nabla f(x+tz)\rVert_{2}\lvert\langle z,v_{2}\rangle\rvert\phi(z)\differential z

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\bigl(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}\bigr)+c_{s}t^{s}\sqrt{\int\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2s}\phi(z)\differential z}\right\},

where we have used the triangle inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the first inequality;
the second inequality follows from the estimate (
49
)
\langle z,v_{2}\rangle
being a standard normal variable, and again the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (for the integral).

Similarly, from the third equation, additionally taking any
v_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\lVert v_{3}\rVert_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\lvert\langle\nabla^{3}f_{t}(x),v_{1}\otimes v_{2}\otimes v_{3}\rangle\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left\lvert\int\langle\nabla f(x+tz),v_{1}\rangle\bigl(\langle z_{,}v_{2}\rangle\langle z,v_{3}\rangle-\langle v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\bigr)\phi(z)\differential z\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{t^{2}}\left\{\int(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}+c_{s}t^{s}\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{s})\bigl\lvert\langle z_{,}v_{2}\rangle\langle z,v_{3}\rangle-\langle v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\bigr\rvert\phi(z)\differential z\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}+c_{s}\sqrt{\int\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2s}\phi(z)\differential z}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\int\lvert\langle z_{,}v_{2}\rangle\langle z,v_{3}\rangle-\langle v_{2},v_{3}\rangle\bigr\rvert^{2}\phi(z)\differential z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\left(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s}+c_{s}\sqrt{\int\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2s}\phi(z)\differential z}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\langle v_{2},v_{3}\rangle^{2}+\lVert v_{2}\rVert_{2}\lVert v_{3}\rVert_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{t^{2}}\left\{(1+c_{s}\lVert x\rVert_{2}^{s})+c_{s}t^{s}\sqrt{\int\lVert z\rVert_{2}^{2s}\phi(z)\differential z}\right\},

where the second equality follows from Isserlis’ theorem.
∎
